## Role mapping
| Role | Agent / substitute |
|---|---|
| DEV | Sonnet 5 subagents (`general-purpose`, `model: sonnet`), one task each, given only the files the task names; the main model (Fable 5.1) writes the brief, reviews the diff, runs Verify and commits |
| DEV — [HIGHER MODEL] T-11, T-16, T-23, T-27 | done by the main model (Fable 5.1) itself, not delegated downward (E.6) |
| DES | literal `spec/madar/03` with tokens read from `apps/web/tailwind.config.ts` (E.2); `design-critic` agent reviews the UI before each gate that ships screens |
| REV | self-review against `06` §1–3 + OWASP written here (E.2), plus the `code-review` skill on each milestone diff; `erp-architect` for any question that touches NPI boundaries |
| TEST | Jest + Playwright per task; project skill `npi-erp-testing` at every milestone gate to prove NPI itself is unchanged |
| SEC | `npm audit --audit-level=high` + secret scan + OWASP list; `security-review` skill at M1, M3 and M5 gates |

Sonnet agent from a closed brief, reviewed by the main model (tsc, eslint, full jest 2419/2419, invariants/no-bypass/npi-client/docs 109/109, Playwright `e2e/setup.spec.ts` 12/12 on the e2e NPI copy). **Design**: `src/onboarding/discover.ts` (`discoverFromNpi`, `guessProfile`, weight table `PROFILE_WEIGHTS`: +3 per pack module a non-admin active role holds, −1 per family the pack lacks, 6 × Jaccard of item categories, 4 × share of the pack's roles present, ±4/−3 for projects, +1 customers/CRM, +1 vendors/procurement; tie → `epc`; confidence high ≥ 30 % lead, medium ≥ 10 %, else low), `summary.ts` (six cards), `SummaryScreen.tsx` («اعتمد وأسّس», «غيّر» per card, «أفضّل ملء الخطوات يدوياً»), `plan.ts` skips roles/departments NPI already holds and notes drifted rules when step 3's source is `npi`, `machine.ts` tracks `sources` per step ('npi' / 'pack-default' / 'heuristic' / 'manual'), `service.ts` runs discovery once on a fresh session, `FirmConfig.setupSource` + `discoveryJson` + reserved `lastSyncAt` / `lastSyncDiffJson` / `lastSyncSeenAt` (migration `0010_auto_setup`, additive), directory reader returns `departmentId`. No model call, no new client method, no new env. **Finding on the way**: the first full copilot jest after the CRM merge had 7 failures in `tests/catalog.spec.ts` — NPI's catalog is 40 keys since CRM phase 1 (5 new: `crm.customer.merge`, `commercial.proposal.approve`, `projects.project.approve`, `scm.procurement.approve`, `finance.costs.view`); the six packs' admin role and the test were topped up (`739b401`). **Seen on the e2e copy of the demo org**: 4 departments, 6 positions, 7 holders, but every position department-less because no seeded NPI user has a department; profile guess `epc` 28.8 vs `general_contracting` 25.2 → «ثقة متوسطة». Spec deviation recorded (02 §Setup asks Q1–Q6) |
| T-47b | done — organization setup kept in step with NPI: sync on every administrator login (10-min throttle) and on «حدّث من NPI» in /settings; banner until dismissed | `964b06b` | 2026-10-10 (night), post-package. Sonnet agent from a closed brief (scratchpad `brief-t47b.md`), reviewed by the main model: tsc, eslint, full jest 2451/2451 (32 new), invariants/no-bypass/npi-client/docs/security 135/135, Playwright desktop settings + setup + auth + smoke 36/36 on the e2e NPI copy. **Design**: `src/onboarding/sync.ts` `syncFromNpi` — skips in order (`not_set_up`, `throttled` within 10 min of `lastSyncAt` for a login, `permission` when roles/departments/directory are blocked, `unreadable` when discovery falls back to pack defaults); the throttle slot is claimed atomically (`updateMany` on `lastSyncAt`) so two administrators logging in together sync once, and is given back on skip or error; reads only roles + departments + directory with the caller's client; reconciles `positions` / `position_assignments` keyed by (roleKey, department): new positions, titles rebuilt only when the stored title still equals the one built from the previous run's `namesJson` (a typed title is never overwritten; renames are detected from the second sync on), new holders, assignments ended (never deleted) with reason `inactive` / `removed` / `moved` (a move creates the new assignment in the same run), emptied positions listed once; writes a `sync_runs` row (`diffJson`, `namesJson`, `skipped`), bumps `lastSyncAt`, and `lastSyncDiffJson = {…diff, at}` only when the diff is non-empty. `src/auth/session.ts` `createSession` runs it for callers holding `admin.department.manage`, awaited with a 10 s bound, every error caught and logged, login never fails because of it. `src/settings/sync.ts` + `SyncCard.tsx` («المزامنة مع NPI»: last sync in Arabic relative time via `i18n/format.ts` `formatAgo`, the diff's lines, «حدّث من NPI» → `POST /api/settings/sync`, manual bypasses the throttle). `SyncBanner.tsx` on the home screen, decided and rendered on the server (no layout shift), «تم» → `POST /api/settings/sync/seen`; shown while `lastSyncSeenAt` is older than the diff's own `at` (not `lastSyncAt`, so a later empty sync does not resurrect a dismissed diff — the one deviation from the brief, covered by a test). Migration `0011_sync_runs` additive. **Risks noted for the demo server**: the first sync after deploy ends every stale assignment (expect one long first banner); rename detection starts one sync late; a thin directory yields `unreadable` silently (visible in /settings and `sync_runs`, not as a banner); admin login waits up to 10 s once per 10 minutes; no transaction around the writes (idempotent, converges on the next run) |

## Milestone summaries
> Phase 0, M1, M2 and M3 summaries moved to `docs/COPILOT_PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md` (2026-10-08) to keep this file small; nothing was rewritten.

### M4
- T-29b (source permission on memory facts, D-17) 2026-10-06: what NPI refuses a user to read, the memory no longer tells them — every NPI-derived fact names the permission of its source and both chat readers and the handover filter by the caller's permissions; chat-stated facts stay as they were. Verified on the real database and the local demo data. Next: T-32 (PII eval, 100 NPI-vocabulary sentences; `tests/no-bypass-llm.spec.ts`).
- T-32 (PII eval + no-bypass-llm guard, F-16) 2026-10-06: the gate is now measured — 100 NPI-vocabulary sentences, names 93.8 % and structured identifiers 100 % against 90/95 — and the two defects the measurement found are fixed («على» opening a person capture; abbreviated honorifics). The structural guard makes the type-level promise greppable: nothing reaches the model except through the pseudonymizer and, for images, through consent → `vision.ts`. One laundering cast in the setup parser closed. Next: T-33 (`/trust` audit table incl. images with the consent id).
- T-33 (the trust screen `/trust`, F-17) 2026-10-08: the administrator now sees every `llm_audit` row of the organization — what left the building (redacted, with the placeholders counted), the tier, tokens and cost, and for every photo/scan send the consent event that authorised it; the events that never called a model (refusal, cap, execution, reassignment) sit in the same table. The integrity line «كل إرسال صورة مسجَّل برقم موافقة» is computed over the whole log (G-M4-2). Verified on the real local log and by the e2e gate. Next: T-34 (`src/audit/cost.ts`, `src/queries/usage.ts`, per document/flow/day/tier + cap).
- T-34 (the usage counter, F-18) 2026-10-08: the cost of every model call is now countable — per Riyadh day, tier, flow, document and user over `llm_audit`, with a monthly projection — and the daily cap is a queryable status that uses the gate's own arithmetic (`capStatus` is what `src/llm/client.ts` calls before refusing, and `t('usage.cap.reached')` is the one text of the refusal). G-M4-3 met: `cost` tests 116 green, the cap test refuses with the cap message exactly where the counter says reached. Verified on the real log: the counter reproduces the `daily_usage` accumulator to the micro-USD. Next: T-35 (`/metrics`, a1–a6 cards incl. a3 from `loadUsageReport`, CSV export).
- T-35 (the metrics screen `/metrics`, F-19) 2026-10-08: the six assumptions of `01` §1 are now measured from Madar's own records on one screen — daily return (`daily_usage` over the Riyadh working week), memory accuracy and PII effectiveness (the latest `eval_runs` of each kind), inference cost per document and per day with the projection (T-34's readers), chat entry (fields correct without edit over the five flows' latest extraction evals, and the approval time against the manual baseline entered in /settings) and onboarding time (T-20's metric) — each with its target, verdict and detail lines, and one `copilot-metrics.csv` with the G-M4-4 header and six assumption rows. G-M4-4 met: `e2e/metrics.spec.ts` green. Verified on the real local data and the running screen. Next: T-36 (`eval:extract` over all 20 documents, `docs/COPILOT_EVAL_AR.md`, `eval:behaviour`, tag `copilot-v0.4.0`).
- T-36 (the extraction eval on the full set + `docs/COPILOT_EVAL_AR.md`, F-20) 2026-10-08: the set now has what `03` §6 asked and never had — two scanned contracts and a photographed one — and the whole set reads at **99.0 % of fields (479/484), 0 status mismatches, 2 confirmed-but-wrong**, every image under a consent id, $1.25 a run. AT-17's text-PDF p90 is 28.4 s on two-page documents (provider time) → D-21 proposes 30 s as D-19 did for AT-01. Next: T-37.
- T-37 (M4 gate: behaviour eval, OWASP, secrets) 2026-10-08: the twenty behaviours of `06` §3 are now a repeatable measurement through the product's own turn runner with deterministic checks — **18/20** (gate 18): the chat cannot answer «كم استهلكنا اليوم؟» with figures (no usage tool yet) and a sentence that names a customer by a redacted core leaves the customer empty (both logged for the owner). Secrets scan clean over the whole history; audit findings all in NPI's stack, none under the copilot. **G-M4-5 met; `copilot-v0.4.0` tagged.** Next: M5 — T-38 (camera and microphone on mobile, `--project=mobile`).
### M5
- T-38 (camera and microphone on mobile, F-21) 2026-10-08: a phone can now photograph a document straight into the existing upload → consent → intake path (oversized photos are shrunk in the browser first) and dictate a sentence into the composer in Saudi Arabic — the words stay a draft the user reads and sends, so nothing new reaches a model. The administrator's seven bottom tabs fit a 390 px screen (they overflowed by 25 px before, unseen by every scroll test). **G-M5-1 met: `--project=mobile` four full runs after the change, on a machine that was also running the CRM worktree's acceptance script and two other dev servers: run 2 88 passed / 1 failed (an ambiguous locator in setup.spec, fixed; that spec 9/9 on its re-run) / 14 skipped (the @live and desktop-only specs); run 3 88 / 1 (auth.spec «wrong password»: page.goto aborted at 35 s on the first navigation; green in runs 2 and 4) / 14; run 4 87 / 2 (not-found and smoke: 30 s navigation timeouts under load; both green in runs 2 and 3) / 14 — every test green in at least one run and no product or assertion failure left; one all-green run on a quiet machine still to be recorded.** The baseline run exposed two environment faults outside T-38 and both are fixed: the e2e NPI api now runs on its own database copy (the shared `npi_erp` carries CRM-branch rows this branch cannot read) and the M1 smoke spec was still asserting the pre-T-26 fixed reply. Open for the owner: D-22 (the browser's speech service). Next: T-39 part 2 (the /copilot layout shift and mobile INP; gate G-M5-2 not met in the first run).
- T-39 (performance, NFR) 2026-10-09: the copilot now has a performance gate — `npm run perf` builds the production bundle, starts it, loads every screen three times cold on a simulated desktop and on a simulated mid-range phone (Lighthouse's throttling), and judges LCP / INP / CLS / TTFB and the JavaScript each route downloads against the budgets in `next.config.js`; eight database indexes for the reads the screens make on every load (migration `0009_indexes`). The gate found and fixed two real defects: the chat screen shifted its content by almost a full viewport after load (the intake cards grew after the scroll to the bottom — CLS 0.94, now 0.002), and every keystroke re-rendered the whole thread. **G-M5-2 not met: 60/64 budget lines pass; the four misses are on the throttled phone profile and share one cause — the chat history, settings and setup fetch their main content after hydration (LCP 3.4–4.7 s against 2.5 s; the same screens 0.7–0.8 s on desktop). Fix proposed as D-23 (server-provided first data, the same pattern the cards now use); the owner decides whether it is done before T-40.** Next: T-40 (Docker image + compose) unless D-23 is taken first.
- T-39b (D-23, performance) 2026-10-09: the three screens now arrive with their content in the HTML — the chat's latest conversation with its cards, the settings view, the setup step (and step 7's dry run) are read on the server through the same code the API routes use, and the panels start ready. The four D-23 budget lines are green (phone LCP: chat 4.7 → 1.9 s, settings 3.5 → 1.3 s, setup 3.4 → 1.0–1.4 s). The gate then exposed four more things the first data made visible and all are fixed: the server-rendered thread jumping to its end at hydration, `PreviewCard` laying out phone-first on a desktop, the tabs' idle-time prefetch under the first key press, and a loading state the gate did not wait for. **G-M5-2: 63 of 64 lines after D-24 (layout shift 0.000 on every line). The last line — trust on the phone, INP 248 against 200 — is the arrival of the eight font files on the throttled link, proven by a run with the fonts blocked (INP 48); fewer font files or no preload is the lever, the owner's D-25.** The full e2e suite is green (182 passed, 22 skipped by design). Next: T-40.
- T-40 (Docker image + compose) 2026-10-09: the copilot has its image (`apps/copilot/Dockerfile`: Node 24 on Alpine, two stages, runs as the unprivileged `node` user, a health probe, migrates its own database on boot) and its place in the production stack (`docker-compose.prod.yml` service `copilot` with the `copilot_docs` volume and an environment of its own — none of NPI's secrets; Caddy routes `/copilot*` to it). The image was built and run locally against an empty Postgres: it created and migrated `npi_copilot`, answered the probe with 200, and lost / regained 200 with the database. Found and fixed on the way: pdf-parse's page rasterizer (`@napi-rs/canvas`) was not traced into the standalone output — the T-21 pending check. **G-M5-3 met locally** (compose config valid, image builds, non-root, health 200); its live half is T-43. Next: T-41 (deploy.sh, backup.sh, demo reset).
- T-41 (deploy scripts, F-22) 2026-10-09: one `sh deploy/deploy.sh` now carries the copilot with NPI — its image built and tagged, its database migrated before any container is swapped, its container swapped after api and web (or started on a server that never ran it), Caddy's routes reloaded so `/copilot*` reaches it, and both health probes checked through Caddy; rollback and `--dry-run` cover it. The nightly backup takes the copilot's database and its uploaded documents with NPI's dump, verified the same way; the demo reset wipes both. Rehearsed against the T-40 image and an isolated Postgres (migrate, dump, archive, reset beside a live copilot — health 200 after). Not done: the first real run on the demo server (T-43; the server's `deploy/.env` needs the two copilot secrets first). Next: T-42 (`docs/COPILOT_RUN_AR.md`, changelog) and T-44 (security headers) in parallel.
- T-42 (owner run guide + changelog, F-22) and T-44 (security headers, SEC) 2026-10-09, in parallel by two Sonnet agents from closed briefs: the owner has a seven-section Arabic guide (`docs/COPILOT_RUN_AR.md`) that quotes the UI's consent strings and NPI's nine refusal messages verbatim and a changelog per tag, both held to «Arabic-only outside code spans» by a test with a declared allowlist; and every copilot response now carries a Content-Security-Policy with a per-request nonce (`'strict-dynamic'`, no `'unsafe-inline'` for scripts), nosniff, frame denial, a referrer policy, camera/microphone limited to the copilot's own origin and HSTS over HTTPS — proven by 26 unit tests and a full desktop browser run with no CSP violation. Not done: the production policy (no `'unsafe-eval'`) seen in a real browser — T-45's full run and T-43 on the server. Next: T-45 (full regression) locally; T-43 and T-46 after the owner prepares the server.
- T-45 (full local regression, REV+TEST) 2026-10-09, main model alone: every suite and every eval of the package is green on the tree as pushed — NPI 198 unit + 31 integration tests, the copilot's 2383 jest tests, Playwright desktop + mobile 184 passed / 0 failed / 22 skipped, no secret in 221 commits, PII gate 93.8 % names / 100 % identifiers, memory 96 %, extraction 98.6–100 % per family with two confirmed-wrong fields in 35 documents (one item name shortened, one phone digit misread from a scan), and the performance gate on the production build 64/64 budget lines with the trust screen's mobile INP inside its budget this run. First time the production Content-Security-Policy (nonce, `'strict-dynamic'`, no `'unsafe-eval'`) ran in a real browser: `scripts/csp-sweep.mjs` (kept for T-43 against the server URL) saw 0 refusals over every screen on desktop and a Pixel 7. One run-order mistake of the session, recorded as a gotcha: `eval:extract` must not overlap a Playwright run (the e2e run wipes `test-results/`, where the eval keeps its scan pages) — the contract family was re-run alone after deleting the eval organization's document rows. Not done: anything on the server (T-43: deploy, `@live-demo`, HTTPS sweep, phone prompts, D-11; T-46: PR, tag, deploy). M5 is complete locally; the branch is release-ready pending the owner's server preparation.
- T-43 (first live deploy, M5) 2026-10-10, main model with the owner at the keyboard: the copilot is live on the demo server (`https://204-168-182-109.sslip.io/copilot`), deployed by one `sh deploy/deploy.sh` beside NPI with the CRM release — backup first, migrations, `api-6` / `web-6` swapped, the copilot started for the first time, Caddy reloaded, both health probes green through Caddy (the first real run of PR #7's `verify()`). The first attempt failed in the api image (280 TypeScript errors): the server was running the CRM release that `main` took on 2026-10-09 (PR #9) and `feat/copilot` was cut before it, and unpacking the tarball over the old tree had mixed the two. The owner chose to merge the CRM branch into `feat/copilot` (`26acb7c`, gates green) rather than start the copilot container alone beside a mixed tree. Live evidence: `@live-demo` 4/4 in 26.7 s over HTTPS (health, production CSP, administrator login with a secure cookie, NPI's sidebar link into the copilot — D-11 closed without a code change) and the CSP sweep with 0 violations and HSTS. Every «Pending live verification» of T-40/T-41/T-43/T-44 is closed; what remains on the server is a phone's camera/microphone prompt, a real upload through the container and a restore rehearsal. **G-M5-3 met live.** Next: T-46 (PR `feat/copilot` → `main`, tag `copilot-v0.5.0`).
- T-46 (release, M5) 2026-10-10: `feat/copilot` merged into `main` through PR #10 (`9aae4f2`) and tagged `copilot-v0.5.0`; the repository CI was green and the server already ran the tree. **M5 complete; the binding package T-01…T-46 is done.** What remains is operational, outside the package: the first copilot night in the backups, a phone's camera/microphone prompt, a restore rehearsal, and the deploy-procedure suggestions (tarball extraction, superset check).

## NPI API gaps found (from onboarding apply and execution)
| Gap | Flow | Proposed NPI change (for the engineer) |
|---|---|---|
| Most response bodies are untyped in the OpenAPI document (71 of 177 operations declare a body, nearly all as a bare object) | all | response DTO classes or `@ApiOkResponse` on the endpoints the copilot reads; until then the copilot narrows responses with hand-written readers that cite the NPI source line |
| `GET /auth/me` and `POST /auth/login` do not return the user's department | positions (T-06) | include `departmentId` in both. Checked at T-06: `/admin/departments` and `/admin/departments/directory` both require `admin.department.manage` (`departments.controller.ts`), so an ordinary employee cannot read their own department. Until NPI returns it, the copilot resolves the position from its own assignment table (filled by the admin during setup, M2), then from the directory when the user holds that permission, and otherwise by role alone |
| `POST /auth/login` is throttled per IP (5 per minute, `auth.controller.ts`), and every copilot login reaches NPI from the copilot server's single address | login (T-05) | none required for the demo; for production either raise `AUTH_THROTTLE_LIMIT` for the copilot's address or have NPI trust a forwarded client address from the copilot. NPI's 429 is shown as «محاولات كثيرة» |
| Query parameters `search`, `status`, `take`, `skip` are documented as required although the controllers treat them as optional | lists | mark them optional with `@ApiQuery({ required: false })` |
| PO, receipt and vendor bill have no generic `/actions` endpoint (the package assumed one); they expose explicit endpoints (`…/approve`, `…/send`, `…/receive`, `…/inspect`, `…/accept`, `…/match`, `…/schedule`) | po, grn, vendor_bill | none — the execution plans in T-27 use the explicit endpoints, never the approve ones |
| No endpoint lists vendor bills, and an inbox task carries the workflow state's name (`stateEn`) but not its code | home tile «فواتير موردين بانتظار المطابقة» (T-08) | a list endpoint for vendor bills with a status filter, and `stateCode` on the inbox task. Until then the tile counts inbox tasks with `documentType === 'VendorBill'` and `stateEn === 'Recorded'`, which is scoped to the caller's role and breaks if the state is renamed in NPI's workflow data |
| No call adds a milestone to an existing contract; milestones are accepted only inline at creation | customer_contract | none needed for the package — extraction must carry milestones before the create call |

(b) Fewer files — drop the `latin` subset (digits and codes then come from the system font) and/or weights 500/600 (`font-medium` → regular, `font-semibold` → bold in 82 places): fonts arrive before the first paint more often, but not always. The gate's new long-task log puts the keys at 3.8–4.5 s, when the eight preloaded IBM Plex Sans Arabic files (≈199 KB) finish on the 1.6 Mbps link; the same screen with the font requests blocked (`--block .woff2`) measures INP 48 (input delay 1–6 ms against 265–419). A font file's arrival costs main-thread time (decode and a style pass) even under `display: 'optional'` — about 250–400 ms in all at 4× CPU, once per cold visit on every screen; only on trust does it coincide with the gate's interaction window. Not a defect of the screen. | (a) D-24's option (b): fewer files — drop the `latin` subset (digits and codes then come from the system font; four files) and/or weights 500/600 (82 call sites): halves or quarters the arrival cost, visible on warm caches too. (b) `preload: false` on `next/font`: in principle no visual change on a warm cache (the cached font is at hand within `optional`'s block period) and a cold visit fetches only the weights and subsets the page's text uses — but a slow disk cache could miss the block period and show the system font on a warm visit, so it needs a warm-cache check on a real phone before it is trusted. (c) Accept the line as a known limit of the lab profile: a one-time delay during a cold load, while field INP is the page-lifetime 98th percentile. Recommended: (c) now, and (b) measured on a real phone in T-43's live gate. | P2 | **decided** (2026-10-09, the owner delegated the call — «قرر أنت الأفضل»: **(c) accepted as a known limit of the lab profile, recorded in the gate itself** — `perfBudgets.knownLimits` in `next.config.js` names the line (trust, mobile, INP), the decision, a **ceiling of 500 ms** (the top of web.dev's «needs improvement» band) and T-43 as the task that re-decides it; `evaluateBudgets` keeps the gate green for that line between budget and ceiling and marks it `accepted` — printed and recorded as over budget, never as a pass — and fails it beyond the ceiling like any other line, so a real regression on the screen is still caught. The budget itself stays 200. (b) `preload: false` was not applied: on a warm cache it trades a reliable brand font for a cold-load delay that field INP (page-lifetime p98) rarely sees, and the local gate cannot do the warm-cache check on a slow disk — T-43 measures it on a real phone against the live server before it is trusted. (a) fewer files was not applied: dropping `latin` puts digits and codes in the system font next to Plex, and 500/600 touch 82 call sites, for a gain the lab line does not need. Tests `tests/perf-lib.spec.ts` «known limits (D-25)».) |
| D-26 | Madar's chat role (owner review, 2026-10-13 morning, after his first live session on the demo server at 06:22): the chat holds exactly the six read tools of `02` §6, so «كم عقد متأخر؟» was answered «لا عقود متأخرة» with no contract tool behind it, and «أريد تقرير المبيعات» was answered by the model as a refusal about employee reports — the HR guard did **not** fire (verified locally: `route` → `chat`, `isHrEvaluationRequest` → `false`), the model improvised from the BR-06 prompt rule because no report tool exists and the prompt says nothing about what to do when a report or a figure is asked for. NPI exposes `/finance/summary`, `/finance/reports/*` (income statement, balance sheet, trial balance, VAT return, profitability, POC, general ledger), `/invoices`, `/reference/dashboard`, `/tasks/summary`; none is reachable from the chat. Memo with the full analysis: `docs/COPILOT_ROLE_REVIEW_AR.md`. | (b) in the memo: keep the security model (reads with the user's own token, writes only through the preview card), add an explicit «I have no tool for that; NPI's screen … has it» rule to `prompts/assistant.md` plus a fixed capability answer, and extend the read tools over NPI's existing endpoints (`get_contract`, `finance_summary` first; `get_report`, `get_requisition`, `get_vendor`, `usage_today` after). A deviation from `02` §6 (tool list) to be recorded here before any code. Alternatives: (a) the prompt rule alone; (c) new write flows (B-01, deferred by the owner) | P1 | **open — the owner's call** (three questions in the memo §7: (a) or (b); whether financial figures are spoken in the chat or only referred to the screen; the definition of an «overdue contract») |

## Known limits (measured later)
- Live flows e2e (T-31): a bare photo or PDF attached with an empty composer is answered with the five flow chips, not an extraction (the classifier is not asked about an empty text); `09` 3.7–3.9 read as if the photo alone starts the bill — the owner presses «فاتورة مورّد» first. A decision for M4 (classify by document kind when the text is empty) rather than a defect.
- Live flows e2e (T-31): the Word-PDF ligature flip («للمقاولات» → «للمقاوالت») still reaches the card on `contract-01.pdf`; it is flagged (`extract.warn.ligature`) and the user corrects it inline. The customer is then created with the corrected name; an uncorrected approve would create it misspelled.
- Live flows e2e (T-31): every run leaves real records in both local databases (D-T28-3): a purchase order and an accepted receipt per AT-04 run, a purchase order and a DRAFT receipt per AT-05 run, a requisition in the matrix per AT-03 run, a contract (and on the first run a customer `CUS-00002`) per AT-01/AT-02 run, plus the copilot's documents, jobs and audit rows. A project «مشروع الموقع الشمالي — مَدار» was opened once from a DRAFT contract (now ACTIVE). The demo reset wipes them.
- Live flows e2e (T-31): the `list` reporter does not write attachments of passing tests to disk — the model replies, card notes and latencies of a green run are read back from `llm_audit` / `intake_jobs` instead (the reviewer's query); switch to the `html` reporter for a run whose attachments must be kept.
- Live flows e2e (T-31): `GET /contracts` cannot say whether a contract already has a project, so `ensureProject` reads `/projects` and excludes their `contractId`s; a contract activated by a run that then failed to open its project is picked up by the next run (ACTIVE-without-project fallback).
- Memory permissions (T-29b): the filter is per retrieval. A fact an earlier, permitted turn put into a conversation stays in that conversation's stored messages — fine within one user's own conversations (permissions rarely shrink), and an administrator reading a predecessor's chat (BR-07) reads what that predecessor was allowed to see at the time. Rows `refreshNpiMemory` did not write (no `valueJson.key`) are neither stamped nor swept; T-29 wrote none such.
- Upload dedupe (D-T31-11): identical bytes uploaded by two users of one organization share one storage path and two rows; deleting one row must not delete the file while the other exists (nothing deletes documents today).
- Execution (T-27): a process that dies between the claim and the final write leaves the job `executing` with a `running` step forever (the card shows «جارٍ»); nothing times it out. The log is truthful up to the last persisted step, and `npiEntityRefs` is written only at the end — the created ids are on the `done` steps in the meantime. A «stale running» rule (age > N minutes → shown as unknown) is a later decision.
- No-bypass e2e (T-28): every run leaves a real `PENDING_APPROVAL` requisition (12,800) with the pm, a failed contract job for the pm, and an empty conversation per seeded user at the top of that user's home chat (`startIntake` needs a conversation id; the home page opens the latest conversation). Nothing is deleted (D-T28-3). The demo reset wipes them.
- No-bypass e2e (T-28): the first browser test after a cold `next dev` waited 20 s for the chat bundle to compile (the server-rendered tiles were there, the composer was not); the composer wait now allows 90 s. Other specs may show the same on a cold start.
- No-bypass e2e (T-28): the `@live-no-bypass` test records the model's reply through `testInfo.attach`, which the `list` reporter does not write to disk — the reply text of the one live run was not kept (the assertions on it passed: `kind: 'answer'`, no job, requisition untouched).
- Invariants (T-28): the environment-name allowlist is exact (16 names); adding any `process.env` read fails the suite until it is listed with a reason — intended, but noisy for a new reader. In development the copilot process also sees NPI's `DATABASE_URL` and `JWT_SECRET` through the shared root `.env` (`dotenv -e ../../.env`); the invariants prove the code never reads them, not that the process cannot — T-40's compose service must not share NPI's `env_file`.
- Execution (T-27): the claim's conditional update treats **any** rejection as «the job is no longer approved» (`state` conflict), including a database outage — the message is then misleading. Mapping only Prisma P2025 needs the Prisma error class inside intake code (kept out so far).
- Execution (T-27): `po_convert` pairs quoted lines with requisition lines by item id only (first unused line of that item); a quote that re-prices one item across two requisition lines gets the prices in quote order.
- Execution (T-27): a chosen PO-line *candidate* (T-26 limit) is executed with `isSerialised: false`; NPI's own check («Line X is serialised: N serial numbers required») is what the user sees then, verbatim.
- Extraction (T-27 live, contract-02 again): `customer.nameAr` came back «الطرف الأول» at confidence 1 despite the T-26 prompt rule — confirmed-but-wrong a second time. Harmless here (the VAT match picked CUS-00001 and the plan used `customer_use`), but with no VAT match the execution would **create** a customer named «الطرف الأول». A server-side rule (role words → non-confirmable warning) is in Suggestions.
- Dev tooling (T-27): `npx playwright test` also starts its own NPI API with `nest start` in `apps/api`, which rebuilds `apps/api/dist` under a developer's `nest start --watch` on :4000 — the watcher restarted mid-rebuild and died with `Cannot find module 'apps/api/dist/main'` (seen 2026-10-05; the first live probe then failed at login with «NPI غير متاح»). Stop the :4000 api before an e2e run, or give the e2e api its own `outDir` (Suggestions).
- Preview (T-26): the preview reads the model's output once; a sentence edit asks the model to apply the change to the *current JSON* and copy everything else verbatim — on the live contract it did exactly that (one field changed, 1,877 output tokens, 12.8 s), but a sentence that names no field returns the JSON unchanged with no message to the user (the card simply does not change). A «لم أفهم التعديل» reply needs the model to say so — a later prompt/schema decision.
- Preview (T-26): «أكّده» vouches for a *reading*, not a value: `contract-01`'s customer name arrives with the lam-alef artifact («للمقاوالت», unconfirmed, `ligature`) and a user who confirms it instead of typing the correct name will create a misspelled customer in T-27. The card offers the pencil for exactly that; a lexicon-based repair stays a later decision (T-23 limit).
- Preview (T-26): choosing a PO-line or receipt *candidate* is stored as a choice but not re-resolved, so no over-receipt / serials warning is computed for that choice until T-27 reads `choices` with the candidates. A `set` or a sentence edit does re-resolve (one NPI list read per entity kind, no cache; 02 §4's 5-minute cache applies to the dictionary only).
- Preview (T-26): the extraction's own `summary` counts every leaf; the card's «{n} مؤكد من {m}» counts only fields that carry a value or are required, so the two differ by the blank optional fields.
- Chat (T-26): an attached document with no words and no keyword goes to «لم أحدد نوع المستند — اختر:» and five chips; the economy classifier (`prompts/router.md`) is still not wired (`deps.classify` undefined), so the chips are the only path for a bare attachment.
- Latency (T-26, live, `claude-sonnet-5`): PR sentence 6.7 s model / 25 s wall in the chat (NPI reads for resolution + first compile of the route); `contract-02` 28 s model (3,450 output tokens, four milestones) / 33 s wall; sentence edit 12.8 s. AT-17's 25 s p90 does not hold for a two-page contract with four milestones read through the chat — the extraction call alone is at the limit (T-23 measured 12–22 s).
- Dev tooling (T-26): `npx playwright test` starts its own `next dev -p 3177` in `apps/copilot`, which shares `.next/` with a developer's `next dev` on :3110 and corrupts it (routes answer 404 until the :3110 server is restarted). Stop the dev server before an e2e run, or give the e2e run its own `distDir`.
- Extraction (contract eval re-run 2026-10-05 after the prompt rule): `contract-03.totalValue` came back unconfirmed (confidence < 0.8) once — a status mismatch, not a wrong value; the field is confirmable on the card.
- Extraction (T-24): a unit word that is also the first word of an item's name is read as the unit — «٥ طقم مكونات مساعدة» came back as qty 5, unit «طقم», item «مكونات مساعدة» (confirmed; the one confirmed-but-wrong field of the pr eval). `resolveItem` then offers «طقم مكونات مساعدة» as a candidate (trigram ≈ 0.8, below the 0.85 auto-match) rather than matching silently, so the user chooses on the card. A lexicon of item names in the prompt would fix it at the cost of sending the catalogue to the model — a T-26/T-29 decision.
- Extraction (T-24): the word-internal reversed ligature after a letter other than «ل» or «ا» («للكابالت» for «للكابلات», po-02) is still unrepairable and shows unconfirmed, as in T-23.
- Extraction latency (T-24, `claude-sonnet-5`, `effort: medium`): 5–9 s per sentence (≈ 6.5k input tokens, of which ≈ 4k the system prompt), 14–22 s per one- or two-page quote (≈ 9.1k input), one 31 s outlier on po-01 in the first run. AT-17's 25 s p90 holds; a five-page quote has not been measured.
- Extraction (T-25): the daily token cap (`DEFAULT_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP` 600k per user) is shared by every eval run of the day; the third grn run of 2026-10-05 hit it (`cap_reached`, one `eval_runs` row at 0.0) and was re-run with `COPILOT_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP=5000000`. Not a defect — the cap is doing its job — but the eval needs its own budget (Suggestions).
- Extraction (T-25, photos): a Latin code printed inside Arabic text may come back with its hyphen displaced («دفعة B-2026-09» was read as «دفعة-B 2026-09» in the first run, confirmed at 0.9); the label word is now stripped server-side and the prompt asks for the code alone, after which the second run read it exactly — but the displacement itself is a model reading error no rule can catch, and a wrong-but-well-formed batch code would pass as confirmed. Serial numbers were read exactly in every photo (18 serials over 4 photos, incl. the 2.5°/quality-55 one).
- Extraction (T-25, photos): a multi-word location («المستودع: موقع المشروع — جدة») came back as «جدة» at 0.7 (unconfirmed) in the first run; the prompt now asks for the whole phrase and the second run read it in full.
- Extraction latency (T-25, `claude-sonnet-5`, `effort: medium`, vision): 7–26 s per one-page photo (≈ 12.6–14k input tokens, of which ≈ 2k the image), 12–16 s per text PDF, 5.5–6 s per sentence; one 46 s / 5.1k-output-token outlier on grn-05 in the first run (not reproduced: 7.5 s / 859 tokens in the second). AT-17's 25 s p90 holds on the second run; the outlier is logged in `llm_audit`.
- Extraction (T-25): a serial range written as a sentence («من BN550-0001 إلى BN550-0200») is not expanded — the prompt makes the model write the two endpoints at ≤ 0.5 so the list is unconfirmed; NPI needs 200 serials for that line, so the receiver must type or scan them (grn-04 of the eval).
- Extraction (T-23): pdf.js reads the lam-alef ligature of a Word-made PDF reversed («للمقاوالت» for «للمقاولات»). `repairPdfText` fixes the two unambiguous shapes («األ…», «اال…»); the word-internal one cannot be told from a genuine «ال» without a lexicon, and the model cannot fix it because the name reaches it as a placeholder. Such a name is therefore shown **unconfirmed** with `extract.warn.ligature` whenever the page showed the artifact (contract-01 in the eval); the user corrects it on the card. A lexicon-based repair, or passing name *candidates* to the model under a different consent, is a later decision.
- Extraction latency (T-23, `claude-sonnet-5`, `effort: medium`): 12–22 s per one- or two-page contract, ≈ 8.2k input tokens of which ≈ 4k are the system prompt. At `effort: high` the two-page contract exceeded the 60 s advanced timeout. AT-17's 25 s p90 holds for these documents; a five-page contract has not been measured.
- Extraction evidence: the prompt asks for evidence on the money/date fields of each milestone only, to keep the reply under ≈ 3k tokens; description fields come back with `page` but no quote.
- Pseudonymizer: a 10-digit commercial-registration number directly followed by «.» («1010567890.») is not replaced by a placeholder (with a tab or space before the period it is). The extraction then copies the digits as printed. Measure in T-32.
- Vision input (T-22): a raw upload of up to 10 MB is accepted by intake, but the provider's limit is 10 MB *base64-encoded* (≈ 7.5 MB raw); a JPEG photo above that is refused by `imageBlock` with `too_large` and nothing is sent — no downscaling exists yet (first real phone photos come with T-38). Scanned PDFs send at most the 5 rasterized pages of T-21. `pdfBlock` (a whole PDF as one `document` block) is built and unit-tested but not used by any flow — `pdf_scanned` goes page by page as images per ADR-27. The daily token cap counts image tokens like text (≈ 1,500 per 1600-px page on Sonnet 5).
- Sensitivity (T-21) errs on the safe side: «العقد غير سري» and "non_confidential_v2.pdf" are refused because the marker word is present; a negation is not understood. Arabic text inside PDFs is checked only when the PDF carries a text layer; a scanned PDF or a photo is checked by file name only (the model never sees it without the T-22 consent anyway). HEIC conversion is covered by unit tests through an injected converter; no real HEIC file has been pushed through `heic-convert` yet (no sample on this machine) — first real photo from an iPhone in T-38 will show it.
- pdf-parse 2.4.5 requires Node ≥ 20.16 (<21) or ≥ 22.3; NPI's containers are `node:20-alpine` (20.x latest satisfies this), the copilot container pins Node 24 per `02` §1 (T-40). Text extraction is run over every page of the PDF (no page cap); rasterizing is capped at 5 pages (`02` §5.2).
- Pseudonymizer, measured by T-32 (2026-10-06, `npm run eval:pii`): names 76/81 (93.8 %), structured identifiers 41/41 (100 %). The five names that pass through are the honest hard cases — a foreign name in Arabic letters («جون سميث»), a family name alone as subject («الدوسري وافق»), a given name not in `names-ar.ts` («علاء الحربي»), an organization with no legal-form word and not in the dictionary («الرمال الذهبية»), «أبو فهد»; without them 76/76. Reported, not gated: 6/155 kept words over-redacted — the seed's role-word user names make «الموقع»/«الحسابات» ⟦موظف⟧ everywhere (a demo-data artifact that any directory with such names reproduces); a heuristic organization span runs up to four words past the legal-form word and swallows what follows («بسعر», «تركيب مكيفات»); the derived core «الرياض» of «مصنع الرياض للصناعات» masks the city; the English cue rule («from», «to») takes «Gulf Solar Panels» / «Riyadh Industries Factory» as ⟦شخص⟧ before the dictionary does and leaves « Co.» visible, then learns «panels» as a person part; a core shared by a customer and a vendor («الجزيرة») is labelled by whichever entry sorts first. Accepted trade-off of the «على» fix: a bare «علي الغامدي» with no title is not captured.
- The assistant has not been run against a real model yet (D-1). Prompts `assistant.md`, `router.md` and `memory-extract.md` are untested in that sense.
- Setup Q1 by free text: a sentence that starts with a legal-form word («شركة طاقة شمسية تعمل …») reaches the model as «⟦جهة_1⟧ …» because the pseudonymizer redacts the whole span; the deterministic multi-profile rule (T-18) covers the sentences that name the activities, and the step-1 cards always work, but a description with no profile phrase in it («شركة تركّب أنظمة على الأسطح») will come back «لم أفهم الإجابة» today.
- Setup model path (T-20 probe, economy model): after the closed key lists were added to the hint, 14 of 15 free-text answers (five step-1 sentences × 2 rounds + the PPA sentence × 5, four step-6 sentences) came back valid; one PPA call in the first round was refused as «لم أفهم» and the reply is not stored in `llm_audit` (only the outbound text is), so the cause is unknown. The screen keeps the administrator on the step; a retry or the card works. `eval/setup-samples.json` carries two model-path sentences, so the live gate can show this rate.
- Second walk for the owner (2026-10-04 21:26–21:28, after a reset of `org_madar_scratch_epc`): 2.1–2.6 as expected again, 12 created / 5 existing / 0 failed in ≈1.5 min, baseline 30 saved. Three artefacts of resetting NPI underneath a copilot database that is not reset: the five holders chosen earlier pointed at user ids that no longer exist, so the apply reported «لم يُحفظ التعيين: المستخدم غير موجود في NPI» ×5 (correct behaviour); the positions card listed each role several times because positions keep the NPI department id of a department that no longer exists and render as «بلا قسم»; the owner's own press on «اعتمد» at 21:21 had already applied the same profile (NPI created the rows then), which is why the first replay showed «موجود 17». NPI's web app could not be opened for row 2.4 on this machine: port 3000 is held by another project's dev server («HR Assistant»), as 3100 was (D-10); the check was done through NPI's API instead.
- Positions of a previous profile linger: re-running setup with a *different* profile writes the new pack's positions but never retires the old ones, so `/settings` lists both sets (seen on a scratch organization that went om_provider → epc: nine positions, four «بلا شاغل»). Holders whose NPI user no longer exists show as «مستخدم غير معروف» (handled). Decision for T-30 (positions/handover): retire positions not in the applied pack, or keep them for history.

- Memory from NPI (T-29): a payment-terms question about a customer that also has contracts and projects gets the project or contract fact ranked above the credit-terms fact (all three name the customer; `plainto_tsquery` is an AND of every word and the trigram score favours the longer sentence). The chat shows the top 3, so the right fact is usually present (eval: 48/50 present, 36/50 first), but the assistant may lead with the wrong one.
- Memory from NPI (T-29): a reply carries its memory notes («ملاحظة من الذاكرة») only when the answer text contains the word «الذاكرة» (`src/agents/run.ts`, T-11). Live: the credit-terms answer said «طبقاً لملاحظات النظام السابقة» and shipped with no note badge although the fact came from memory; the lead-time answer said «من الذاكرة» and carried both notes.
- Memory from NPI (T-29): item names in NPI may hold Latin letters («كابل DC 6مم»), so a recurring-item sentence can carry Latin text against `08` §E.8; the copilot copies NPI's name as is.
- Memory from NPI (T-29): a firm with more than 60 non-cancelled POs has only the 60 most recent read; a vendor whose receipts are all older loses its lead-time fact at the next sweep. Sentences for the demo data's same-day receipts read «أقل من يوم» (test data receives on the approval day).
- Handover (T-30): a predecessor who still holds the role in NPI and signs in again is attached to the position again by `resolvePosition` (NPI is the authority on who holds a role; the copilot cannot end an NPI role). The document and the ended assignment stay as history. In the demo org the e2e fixture resets the pm position to one holder before every run.
- Handover (T-30): a firm with more than 200 PENDING chains loses the oldest (`/approvals/history` is `createdAt desc`, `take` ≤ 200, read once); the document then shows the 50 oldest of those 200. `total` is on the wire and not yet compared — a «showing N of M» line is a cheap follow-up.
- Handover (T-30): a position-scope vendor fact appears both under «الموردون» and under «الإجراءات المعتادة» (routines are every position-scope fact). Cosmetic.
- Handover (T-30): the loser of two concurrent reassignments gets Prisma's serialization error (P2034), which the route maps to 500 «تعذر» — a retry succeeds or answers «يشغل المنصب فعلاً». A 409 mapping is a one-line follow-up.
- Handover (T-30): the `/approvals/history` read, the directory, roles, departments and vendors lists run inside the reassignment request as the administrator; in the live run the model call took 15.6 s (3,492 in / 1,507 out tokens on `claude-sonnet-5`) and the whole request 36 s including the browser round trips.
- Memory from NPI (T-29): the refresh runs inside the first stale chat turn of the day and adds its NPI reads (four lists + up to 60 PO details) to that turn's latency; on the local data it is well under a second.
- Trust screen (T-33): `chat` rows whose outbound text carries `tool_use`/`tool_result` blocks (5 of 23 chat rows on the dev log) render as `raw` — the text is shown whole, not structured — because `AuditOutboundBlock` carries text/image/document only (D-T33-8 falls back rather than drops).
- Trust screen (T-33): the list query reads `outboundRedactedText` in full to report `outboundChars` (Prisma has no length-only select): a page of 100 rows reads several KB per row; the summary (4 counts) and the user list (1 distinct) run on every page incl. every «المزيد».
- Trust screen (T-33): the placeholder count includes the system prompt's own template placeholders (e.g. «⟦تسمية_رقم⟧» in the extraction prompts), so a text extraction shows 3–7 «رموز مخفاة» even when the user text held one name — the count is of what left the building, as decided, not of the user's PII alone.
- Usage counter (T-34): `loadUsageReport` reads every `llm_audit` row of the period into memory and folds it in JavaScript (no SQL `GROUP BY`): 77 rows for the dev log's month, ten columns each — a busy firm's year is a few hundred KB per report; fine for the pilot, a raw aggregate if it ever is not.
- Usage counter (T-34): the projection divides by the calendar days the window touches, not by the days with traffic — a 30-day window over a firm that started last week under-states its run rate, a one-day window over a heavy day over-states it; `01` §7 asks for a monthly projection and gives no rule, so the simple one is documented in `UsageProjection`.
- Usage counter (T-34): `loadCapStatus` is keyed by user only (`daily_usage` is unique per user and day); a caller must pass the session's own user id — there is no organization check to catch a wrong id.
- Usage counter (T-34): the cap is checked against `daily_usage`, which is written after a successful call, so two concurrent calls of one user just below the cap both pass (the T-09 design, unchanged); the counter shows the overshoot afterwards.
- Metrics (T-35): a2/a4/a5-fields come from `eval_runs`, which has no organization column — every organization sees the same latest eval; a run started by anyone (including the e2e fixture, which seeds and removes its own `memory`/`pii`/`extract:po` rows) is «the latest» until the next one.
- Metrics (T-35): a1 counts a user as active from one message in the window and counts only Sunday–Thursday; public holidays are working days to it, and a firm with a different week needs `WORKING_WEEKDAYS` changed (one constant).
- Metrics (T-35): a5's approval time is `intake_jobs.createdAt → approvedAt` — the dev log's 0.6-minute median is test traffic, not a measurement of a real employee; the ratio becomes meaningful with the pilot's own jobs, and it needs a baseline per flow in /settings (today only `customer_contract` has one).
- Metrics (T-35): `loadMetricsInput` reads every `eval_runs` row (newest first) and every approved job of the organization into memory; both are small today, a `take`/date bound if they ever are not.
- Metrics (T-35): the page computes on every request (`force-dynamic`, five reads + the T-34 fold); no cache — fine for an administrator's occasional visit.
- Extraction eval (T-36): no five-page text PDF in the set, so AT-17 is measured on one- and two-page documents (D-21); a scanned PDF sends at most the first five rasterized pages (T-21 `PDF_RASTER_MAX_PAGES`), so a long scanned contract loses its annexes.
- Extraction eval (T-36): the two confirmed-but-wrong fields are both names (an item name without «طقم»; a vendor name with the printed English name in parentheses) — the card does not warn on them; neither touches an amount, a code or a date, and the vendor was matched by VAT anyway.
- Extraction eval (T-36): one full run a day under the eval user's cap; the model's reading of the same image varies slightly between runs (contract-03-scan left `retentionPct` empty this time) — a single run is one sample, not a bound.
- Behaviour eval (T-37): exactly at the gate (18/20); #16 needs a usage read tool and #17 a prompt rule before they can pass; a run is one sample of a live model — #7 passed in runs 3 and 5 by memory notes, and the model's choice of tools (memory_search vs get_customer) varies.
- Behaviour eval (T-37): every run leaves two requisitions (and, once, a customer) in the local NPI demo data, as the live e2e does; the approved one is reused on the next run, the pending one is created again.
- Behaviour eval (T-37): #14's «audit error» is read as «the model calls of that turn are audited and the reply is Arabic»; the NPI failure itself is reported to the model as a tool error, not written as an audit event (there is no such event kind).
- Mobile (T-38): the camera and the recognizer are not exercised by any automated run — a headless Chromium has neither; the e2e feeds the file input and installs a stand-in `SpeechRecognition`. A real iPhone/Android pass (camera, HEIC, dictation quality in Arabic) is a pending live check.
- Mobile (T-38): dictation quality and availability depend on the browser's service (D-22); a flaky connection yields `network` errors shown under the box; no offline path.
- Mobile (T-38): `preparePhoto` re-encodes only what is too big; a 3 MB photo at 1800 px goes up as-is (fine for both limits). `imageOrientation: 'from-image'` is honoured by current Chrome/Safari; an old WebView may store a portrait photo sideways.
- e2e (T-38): `npi_erp_e2e` is a snapshot — rows written later to `npi_erp` (new seed data, CRM data) are not in it until `node packages/db/prisma/madar-e2e-db.mjs --refresh`; the copilot's own `npi_copilot` is still shared with the dev server. The `@live-flows` and no-bypass specs now write their requisitions/customers into the copy, not into the demo data.
- Performance (T-39): the figures come from a development machine that was also running NPI's web and api dev servers and the CRM worktree; single loads vary by 2–3× under 4× CPU throttling (`/copilot/setup` mobile LCP 0.9 s / 3.5 s / 3.8 s inside one gate — the LCP element differs by whether the client-rendered list outgrows the server-rendered heading). The median of three is judged; every load's figures, the LCP element, the layout-shift sources, the slowest events and the fetch waterfall are in the JSON report.
- Performance (T-39): `next/font` loads IBM Plex Sans Arabic in four weights × two subsets = eight files, 195 KB, finishing at 2–4.7 s on the mobile profile; text paints in the fallback font first (`display: swap`), so LCP is not gated by it, but every screen re-paints once when the font arrives.
- Performance (T-39): the indexes are justified by the query shapes and by `EXPLAIN` (each predicate takes its index once sequential scans are disabled); the local tables are too small for a measured gain (309 audit rows, 58 jobs, 169 facts, 5 handover documents) — TTFB is 44–95 ms on every screen before and after. The gain shows only with a year of audit rows.
- Performance (T-39b): the arrival of the eight font files on the throttled phone occupies the main thread ~250–400 ms (decode and the style pass, even under `display: 'optional'`), once per cold visit on every screen; a key pressed in that window waits for it — the trust screen's mobile INP line (248 against 200; 48 with the fonts blocked). D-25 decided: the line is a known limit declared in the gate (`perfBudgets.knownLimits`, ceiling 500); the gate is green with that line marked `accepted` and fails if it ever passes 500. Re-measured live in T-43.
- Performance (T-39b): runs 1–7's JSON reports were deleted by the e2e run (Playwright empties `test-results/`); the final run's report is `apps/copilot/perf-results/2026-10-09T07-54-21-973Z.json` and the gate writes there from now on.
- Docker (T-40): the copilot image is 596 MB (Next standalone + traced server modules, `@napi-rs/canvas` musl 28 MB, the Prisma CLI and its engines); `node:24-alpine` resolved to Node 24.21 in the image against 24.19 on the development machine. `NEXT_PUBLIC_NPI_WEB_URL` is baked at build time (empty → same origin), so splitting the two apps across origins would need a rebuild.
- Docker (T-40): the session cookie is `secure` under `NODE_ENV=production` (`src/auth/cookie.ts`), so the deployed copilot only logs in over HTTPS — `SITE_ADDRESS=:80` (plain HTTP on an IP) would drop the cookie. The demo server runs HTTPS (sslip.io); a plain-HTTP rehearsal of the full stack cannot test login.

## Suggestions (not implemented)
- ~~Execution (T-27): read `previewJson.choices` with the resolution's candidates … write `executionLogJson` step by step …~~ — done in T-27 (`pickedId`, `buildPlan` mirrors `previewSteps`, step-by-step persistence).
- Extraction (T-27 finding): make role words («الطرف الأول», «العميل», «المالك», «المورّد») a server-side non-confirmable warning on `customer.nameAr` / `vendor.nameAr` in `assessCustomerContract` / `assessVendor` (a `role_word` `FieldWarning`), so a prompt slip can never create a party with that name; the prompt rule alone did not hold on contract-02.
- Execution (T-27): a «stale running» rule for jobs left `executing` by a crash (Known limits), and a P2025-only mapping for the claim.
- Execution (T-27): `bill_match_npi` could call NPI's `/bills/{id}/match` (a comparison record, not an approval) and show the variance on the card; left manual in this task — decide with the engineer whether the copilot may record the match.
- Execution (T-27): resolve `locationName` against `GET /procurement/locations` for `grn_create.locationId`; item creation from the card (needs a category/unit prompt) if the owner wants «صنف جديد» back.
- Playwright: give the e2e NPI api its own `outDir` (or build once to a temp dir) so a developer's `nest start --watch` on :4000 survives an e2e run, as `.next/` does for the copilot (T-26 suggestion).
- Trust screen (T-33): `intake_executed` / `intake_execution_failed` rows carry no tokens; show them as actions with the step that failed (`error` column) and link to the job.
- Edit prompt (T-26): let the model mark «لم أفهم التعديل» (a reserved field or a sentinel) so the card can say so instead of showing an unchanged preview.
- Playwright: give the e2e copilot its own `distDir` (or refuse to start when :3110 is up) so a developer's dev server survives an e2e run.
- Playwright (T-28): have the no-bypass fixture reuse the user's latest conversation (or create the job with `conversationId: null` if `startIntake` allowed it) so a run does not push an empty conversation to the top of the engineer's and the pm's home chat.
- Invariants (T-28): broaden the forbidden-literal scan from fixed suffixes to a regex on route segments (`/\/(approve|decide|reject|match|send|inspect|accept)\b/`) once the ShellFrame decision (D-16) settles how non-NPI DELETEs are excused — the current list would miss `/approve?x=1`.
- ~~Logout (T-28 finding): the browser's logout is the copilot's only `DELETE`; switching it to `POST /copilot/api/auth/logout` would let the invariant forbid the verb outright.~~ — done (D-16, `e9e7258`). ~~Left: remove the legacy `DELETE /copilot/api/auth` handler once `e2e/auth.spec.ts` logs out through the POST (T-38).~~ — done in T-38.
- Preview (T-26): the home chat renders every intake card of the conversation in full; after a dozen jobs the page gets long — collapse approved/cancelled cards to their header line.

- Pseudonymizer (T-09): stop an organization span that starts with a legal-form word at a known generic activity word (طاقة شمسية، مقاولات، تشغيل وصيانة، استشارات، تجارة …) so a firm *description* keeps its words while a firm *name* stays redacted; measure against the T-32 sentence set before changing it (reviewer finding 2, T-18).

- `/settings` last-apply card: the «لا تتيحه واجهة NPI حالياً» list shows the permission key and the role key as codes; carry `describeGapAr`'s Arabic sentence in the settings view (`SettingsLastApply.gaps`) so the card reads like the diff card does.

Decide with T-33 (trust screen) whether replies are stored, and under which retention.
- Setup step 1: the step-1 answer carries the revenue lines the sentence named (09 §2.2 «يذكر خطي الإيراد»), but the screens never show them — step 2 opens directly and the Q7 summary lists modules, documents, positions and terms only. Show the chosen profile and its revenue lines on the step-2 header or in the summary.
- The scratch seed reset keeps the copilot's own rows (`firm_config`, `positions`, `position_assignments`, `onboarding_sessions`) for the scratch organizations; a `--copilot-reset` is deliberately not offered (the copilot never runs NPI tooling and vice versa). Clear them by hand if a scratch organization must look brand-new to the copilot too.
- Owner's walk 2026-10-04 (M2 gate, found by the owner): the setup sentence «شركة طاقة شمسية تعمل EPC وتشغيل وصيانة» was typed into the home chat, not `/setup`, and the assistant answered with generic questions. Two gaps: (1) the router should recognise a firm-activity description (or any setup-like sentence from an administrator before the first apply) and answer with a link to «التأسيس» instead of a chat turn; (2) the assistant's replies on that organization leaned on the setup overlay («أنا هنا لمساعدتك في إعداد المنشأة الأولي») and claimed no access to live operations for «ما هي دورة المشتريات الحالية؟» although it has read tools — the overlay wording and the no-answer path need a probe set in M4's `eval/behaviour.json`. No server error behind it (API and copilot logs clean; the wizard itself worked when opened).
- Pseudonymizer (T-32): the identifier rule should accept a trailing «.» or «،» after a 10-digit CR number (see Known limits, T-23).
- Pseudonymizer (after T-32): stop a heuristic organization span at a known generic activity/commerce word («بسعر», «تركيب», «توريد», «قبل») instead of taking four words; let a dictionary match win over the English cue rule (run the candidate pass before the English heuristic, or exclude dictionary surfaces from it); when a core is shared by two kinds, prefer the kind of the full-name match in the same sentence.
- Eval (after T-32): AT-09's live turn is a reviewer's check today — add it as a `@live-flows` scenario in `e2e/scenarios.spec.ts` for T-37 (the M4 gate) so the M4 tag carries it.
- Chat (seen in the AT-09 live turn): asked for the last purchase order with a named vendor, the model looked the vendor up with `get_customer` and answered that it was not found — there is no vendor lookup tool; a `get_vendor` read tool (name/VAT → code, open POs) would close that.
- Extraction (T-26/T-27): `ExtractionResult` is JSON-serializable and is what `intake_jobs.extractedJson` should store; `fields` (dotted paths) is the shape the preview card groups, `values` the shape the execution plan maps through `customerDtoFrom`/`contractDtoFrom`.
- Eval documents: `eval/extract/customer_contract/src/*.html` are the sources; re-render with `scripts/eval-extract-docs.ps1` (Word, Windows) after editing. For T-24/T-25 the same renderer serves quotes and delivery notes; photos for GRN/bill need real images.
- Eval documents (T-24): `eval/extract/po/src/po-0N.html` and `eval/extract/pr/src/pr-08.html` render with `scripts/eval-extract-docs.ps1 -Flow po` / `-Flow pr`; sentence entries live in `expected.json` only (`text` + `referenceDate`).
- Eval documents (T-25): `eval/extract/grn/src/*.html` and `eval/extract/vendor_bill/src/*.html` render with `scripts/eval-extract-docs.ps1 -Flow grn` / `-Flow vendor_bill`; then `npm run eval:extract:photos -- grn vendor_bill` turns every `.jpg` entry's PDF into a tilted, compressed JPEG (page 1 only — keep photo sources to one page; `photo: { rotateDeg, quality }` per entry) and deletes the intermediate PDF. The `.jpg`/`.pdf` files are committed; re-run both steps only after editing a source.
- Eval runs (T-25): the eval user `eval:extract` shares the copilot's per-user daily token cap (600k); a day with several full runs needs `COPILOT_DAILY_TOKEN_CAP` raised for the run (the client already reads it) — consider an `EVAL_` override or a per-run user id in T-36.
- ~~Preview card (T-26): `extract.incomplete` is the contract wording; `extract.incomplete.pr` / `extract.incomplete.po` exist for the other two flows — the card should pick the key by flow. `ExtractionResult.values === null` with a non-empty `assessed.lines` means a line was dropped for a bad quantity: show it with its warning rather than hiding it.~~ — done in T-26 (`incompleteAr` per flow; dropped lines appear as rows with their `quantity` warning).
- Execution plan (T-27): for `po`, `values.sourceRequisitionCode` + `resolveRequisition` → `POST /requisitions/{id}/convert` (`convertToPoDtoFrom`) instead of `POST /procurement/purchase-orders`; for `pr`, `ItemRef.unitCost` gives the estimated total the matrix threshold check needs before NPI sees the request (AT-03: 40 × 320 > 10k → `pm`).
- WBS (gap): NPI exposes no WBS list; `wbsFromProject` depends on `GET /projects/{id}` carrying `wbsElements`. If it does not, `wbsCode`/`wbsName` stay text on the card and `wbsElementId` is never sent — list it under NPI API gaps when T-27 confirms the response shape.

- Memory (T-29): rank hits by kind when the question names the kind («شروط سداد» → `customer_credit_terms`/`contract_payment_terms`, «مدة توريد» → `vendor_lead_time`), or OR the question's content words after dropping question words — would fix the eval's two misses and the top-1 rate.
- Memory (T-29): attach the prompt's memory notes to the reply when the answer repeats the fact's numbers or entity, not only when it says «الذاكرة» (or tell the assistant to use that word verbatim).
- Memory (T-29): permission-tagged facts (D-17); a `memory_sync` row per org instead of the in-process attempt map, so a multi-process deploy does not refresh once per process.

- Handover (T-30, A's finding): chat-learned vendor facts carry no NPI id (`npiEntityId` null) — resolving the vendor at extraction time (T-11) would make the handover's grouping exact instead of name-based.
- Trust screen (T-33): two more `AuditOutboundBlock` shapes (`tool_use`, `tool_result`) in `src/audit/types.ts` and the guards, so chat rows render structured; a «نص المستخدم فقط» placeholder count beside the total; a date range filter and a CSV export of the log (T-35 exports metrics, not the log); link a row's `conversationId` to a read-only transcript (the handover's predecessor view already renders one).
- Usage counter (T-34): T-35's a3 card = `serializeUsageReport(await loadUsageReport(db, { npiOrgId }, riyadhTrailingPeriod(now, 30)))` with `byDay`/`byTier`/`byFlow` and the projection, plus `loadCapStatus(db, { npiUserId, cap: dailyTokenCap(process.env), now })` for the signed-in user; a «الاستهلاك» line on `/documents/[id]` from `loadDocumentUsage`; a `usage_today` read tool (copilot DB, like `memory_search`) so «كم استهلكنا اليوم؟» (`06` behaviour scenario 16, T-37) answers with figures — the readers exist, the tool is one `ReadTool` entry.
- Metrics (T-35): a `?days=` window on the page and the export (the windows are constants today: 14 for a1, 30 for a3); a per-flow a5 table instead of detail lines once the pilot has jobs in every flow; a «run the evals» note with the command next to a2/a4/a5 when the latest run is older than N days; an `eval_runs.npiOrgId` column if evals ever run on a firm's own data.
- Extraction eval (T-36): three prompt/assessor rules, each measurable by the next run — (1) a Latin name in parentheses after an Arabic party name goes to `nameEn`, not into `nameAr`; (2) keep an item name as printed when it is in the source text; (3) «بلا محتجزات / بلا دفعة مقدمة» → 0, not empty. A five-page contract source for AT-17 as written.
- Behaviour eval (T-37): a `usage_today` read tool over T-34's `loadUsageReport`/`loadCapStatus` (the T-34 suggestion) makes #16 pass; a prompt line that a placeholder such as «⟦عميل_1⟧», with or without a preceding legal-form word, is a name to copy verbatim makes #17 pass — both touch F-18/F-05 prompts, so they are the owner's call, not the gate's.
- Usage counter (T-34): a `GET /copilot/api/usage` for the administrator (`admin.user.manage`, org-scoped, `?days=7|30`, `?documentId=`) once a screen needs it; today nothing fetches usage over HTTP.
- Mobile (T-38): a «retake» affordance on the consent card (the photo is blurry) that re-opens the camera; a thumbnail of the captured photo in the user bubble; haptic/visual feedback when the recognizer stops by itself after silence.
- e2e (T-38): give the e2e copilot its own `npi_copilot_e2e` the same way (`madar-e2e-db.mjs` can copy both), so a run never pushes conversations and jobs into the developer's own home chat (the T-28 suggestion above becomes moot).
- Performance (T-39): ~~the D-23 change first~~ (done, T-39b), ~~the tabs' prefetch off~~ (done, T-39b). Left: the font files — D-24 decided (`display: 'optional'`: layout shift 0.000 on every line); their arrival cost on the main thread (INP) is D-25, decided: accepted as a known limit with a ceiling; if T-43's live gate still shows it on a real phone, try `preload: false` with a warm-cache check first, and only then weight 500 (10 call sites, two files).
- Performance (T-39b): the gate's figures depend on the organization's data (which step the open setup session is on, how long the chat history is); a seeded, fixed state for the gate's own account would make two runs comparable.
- Performance (T-39): a `--compare <report.json>` for `npm run perf` that prints the deltas against an earlier run, so a regression reads as a number instead of two files side by side.
- ~~Deploy (T-41): extend `deploy.sh` with `$COMPOSE build … copilot`, `docker tag npi-erp-copilot "npi-erp-copilot:$sha"`, a migration step `$COMPOSE run --rm --no-deps copilot node node_modules/prisma/build/index.js migrate deploy --schema apps/copilot/prisma/schema.prisma` before the swap, `swap copilot`, and a verify of `/copilot/api/health` through Caddy beside `/api/health`; rollback tags the copilot image too. The two new required variables must be in the demo server's `deploy/.env` before the first deploy (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `COPILOT_SESSION_KEY`).~~ — done in T-41 (`d315104`); the two variables remain the owner's step before T-43.
- Deploy (T-41): `deploy.sh --rollback` cannot restore an earlier `deploy/Caddyfile` (the server holds an unpacked tarball, not a checkout); keep the previous tarball on the server (`/opt/npi-erp-deploy.<sha>.tar.gz`) or write `deploy/.deploy_history` entries with the tarball name so a route change can be undone by hand.
- Backup (T-41): a restore rehearsal of the copilot (dump + documents archive) on the demo server after the first deploy, timed and recorded like NPI's 2026-08-17 rehearsal; and an `OFFSITE_CMD` example that ships the three files of a night together.
- ~~Deploy (T-40): a full local rehearsal of `docker-compose.prod.yml` (api + web + caddy + copilot on `:80`) before T-43 would show D-11 (the NPI sidebar's `next/link` to `/copilot`) without waiting for the demo server; it needs ports 80/443 free on this machine (another project's Caddy holds them today).~~ — moot: D-11 was observed and closed on the demo server at T-43.
- Deploy (T-43): the runbook unpacks the tarball over `/opt/npi-erp` with `tar -xzf`, which never deletes a file the new tarball does not carry — an older tree's files survive beside the new ones (on 2026-10-10 the api build saw CRM test files next to a pre-CRM `packages/shared`). Either extract into a fresh directory and switch a symlink (`/opt/npi-erp` → `/opt/npi-erp-<stamp>`, keeping `deploy/.env`, `deploy/.deploy_history` and the volumes outside it), or have the runbook delete `apps/` and `packages/` before extraction; and write the tarball's md5 into `.deploy_history` so a rollback can name what it restores.
- Deploy (T-43): the deploy branch must be a superset of what the server runs; `deploy/pack.ps1` could refuse to pack when the current branch does not contain `origin/main` (`git fetch origin main` then `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD`), which would have stopped the 2026-10-10 first attempt before the upload.
- Deploy (T-43): after the first copilot night, read the server's backup directory once — `npi_copilot` dump + documents archive beside NPI's dump — and time a restore of the copilot (the T-41 suggestion above, now possible).

## Pending live verification
- **TestSprite — asked for by the owner 2026-10-11, postponed by the owner 2026-10-12; no test reached either application.** The MCP route is blocked: `testsprite_check_account_info` answers «Invalid TestSprite API Key» (a new key in the Windows user environment and an app restart are the owner's). The run went through the owner's TestSprite web workspace against the demo server instead: two «Frontend UI» projects, the seeded administrator as the test account (password typed by the owner), «Log in once per run» on, 10 tests at a time — `madar-copilot-demo` on `/copilot` (8 use cases, 52 tests proposed, 24 run) and `npi-erp-demo` (12 use cases, 85 proposed, 17 run). Both runs failed 100 % in TestSprite's own sign-in before the first test («Login failed: still on auth page»), so they say nothing about Madar or NPI. What the server holds: for the NPI project, NPI's `AuditLog` has one `LOGIN` and five `LOGIN_FAILED` (the administrator's e-mail, wrong password) from three TestSprite runner addresses — the same stored credentials succeeded once; for the Madar project, five `LOGIN_FAILED` from the copilot container (`172.18.0.4`) at 03:25:57–03:26:20 UTC during TestSprite's exploration, which tripped Madar's own limit (`src/auth/ratelimit.ts`, 5 failures per 15 minutes per e-mail), so the run at 03:32 UTC was refused by Madar before NPI was called (`login_attempts`: those five rows and nothing after; the lock ended by itself at 03:41 UTC). Why TestSprite's sign-in is rejected most of the time is not established. Cost: 12 of the free plan's 150 credits. To resume: recreate the two projects with TestSprite's live-browser sign-in (offered only in the creation wizard's access step, not in a project's settings), with a dedicated test user rather than the administrator, so that a failing automation cannot lock the administrator out of Madar for 15 minutes; or renew the MCP key and run against local servers with `AUTH_THROTTLE_LIMIT` raised (the shared per-address throttle of the «NPI API gaps» table).

- Observation, not a defect: `09` 1.4 expects «ملاحظة من الذاكرة». Memory notes come only from facts the employee stated earlier (`prompts/memory-extract.md`); on a fresh database `memory_search` returns nothing, so the badge «الذاكرة» shows but no note. The owner should state a fact first (e.g. a payment term agreed with a customer) and ask again.
